      - content:
        - #0:
          - type: reasoning
          - text: Let me analyze the current situation carefully.
            
            ## Critical Events This Turn:
            
            1. **NUCLEAR ATTACK ON AWASA** (Event 100): The Netherlands detonated an Atomic Bomb on Awasa (57,3). Awasa's population dropped from 21 to 12. Multiple units killed, tile improvements pillaged, fallout created on several tiles around Awasa.
            
            2. **Netherlands producing more nuclear weapons**: Amsterdam produced another Atomic Bomb (event 119-121). Netherlands now has at least 2 Atomic Bombs repositioned at Nijmegen (events 188-191), plus a Fighter and Heavy Bomber also rebased to Nijmegen.
            
            3. **Heavy combat losses**: We lost Infantry ID 22718 (killed by Netherlands XCOM Squad near Lisbon), Anti-Aircraft Gun ID 19436 (killed in Awasa zone), Anti-Aircraft Gun ID 19846 (killed near fallout zone), Machine Gun in Awasa nuke zone.
            
            4. **Positive developments**: 
               - We shot down 3 Netherlands Fighters (events 145, 147, 149) - good air defense
               - We killed a Netherlands Infantry (ID 20571) near our territory (event 244-245)
               - Golden Age started (10 turns)
               - Roskilde produced Paratrooper
               - Awasa constructed Military Base
               - Rifleman upgraded to Infantry
            
            5. **World Congress**: Voting in 5 turns now (was 6). Netherlands has 44 delegates (104% of needed 42). This is CRITICAL - Netherlands could win Diplomatic Victory!
            
            6. **Coalition delegate count**: 
               - Mongolia: 11
               - Japan: 13
               - Venice: 14
               - Denmark: 15
               - The Huns: 8
               - Total coalition: 61 (without Indonesia's 7)
               - Netherlands: 44 + city-state delegates from spheres
            
            Wait, let me recalculate. The Netherlands needs 42 delegates. They have 44. But our coalition has Mongolia (11) + Japan (13) + Venice (14) + Denmark (15) + us (8) = 61. If we all vote against Netherlands, we can block them (44 < 42 needed from supporters, and 61 against > 44 for). Wait, actually the voting works differently - delegates vote FOR a proposal. So Netherlands needs 42 delegates voting FOR them. They have 44 of their own. If the coalition all votes against, Netherlands gets 44 FOR and 61 AGAINST. 44 > 42 threshold... but wait, the threshold is for the number of delegates needed. Let me re-read.
            
            "Designate a player that could achieve Global Hegemony. If a Civilization receives support from enough Delegates in the Congress, that leader will attain Diplomatic Victory. The number of Delegates needed depends on the number of Civilizations and City-States in the game."
            
            So Netherlands needs 42 delegates voting FOR them. They have 44. If our coalition (61 delegates) all votes AGAINST, Netherlands still gets 44 FOR which exceeds 42. WE NEED TO REDUCE NETHERLANDS' DELEGATES OR GET MORE OURSELVES.
            
            Wait, but city-states that are allied with Netherlands (Quebec City, Ife, Salem, Yerevan, Bogota, Melbourne, Lisbon) also give delegates to Netherlands. Let me count Netherlands' total more carefully.
            
            Netherlands has 44 delegates listed. The threshold is 42. So they EXCEED the threshold with their own delegates alone! Even if every other civ votes against, Netherlands wins if they vote for themselves with 44 delegates.
            
            This is an EMERGENCY. Netherlands will win Diplomatic Victory in 5 turns unless something changes.
            
            Let me think about what we can do:
            1. We cannot directly reduce their delegate count except by capturing cities or flipping city-state alliances
            2. Netherlands has Sphere of Influence over Quebec City and Ife - these are permanent allies
            3. Netherlands is allied with multiple other city-states too
            
            Actually wait - the World Congress vote. Let me re-read. The proposal is "Global Hegemony" and it's automatically proposed. Netherlands would presumably vote for themselves. But other civilizations can also vote for or against.
            
            The question is: can our coalition's 61 delegates OUTVOTE Netherlands' 44? In Vox Populi, the Global Hegemony vote requires a civilization to receive support from ENOUGH delegates (42). Delegates voting AGAINST don't subtract from the FOR count. So if Netherlands gets 44 FOR votes, they win regardless of how many AGAINST votes there are.
            
            This means the only way to prevent Netherlands' diplomatic victory is:
            1. Reduce their delegate count below 42 before the vote
            2. Repeal World Ideology: Autocracy (which gives extra delegates)
            3. Capture their cities to reduce their base delegate count
            4. Flip their city-state alliances
            
            Wait, where do Netherlands' 44 delegates come from? Let me think:
            - Base delegates from civs + city-states
            - World Ideology: Autocracy gives +1 delegate per Autocracy civ, +1 per civ following Autocracy
            - Sphere of Influence city-states
            
            The Netherlands is Freedom ideology, not Autocracy. But World Ideology: Autocracy is enacted, which benefits Autocracy civs. However, the resolution says "Civilizations following the Ideology get an additional Delegate, plus 1 Delegate for every other Civilization that follows the Ideology."
            
            Let me check who follows Autocracy: Denmark (Autocracy), The Huns (Autocracy), Mongolia (Order actually... wait let me check), Japan (Order).
            
            Actually looking at the players:
            - Denmark: Autocracy (9 policies)
            - The Huns: Autocracy
            - Mongolia: Order (5 policies)  
            - Japan: Order (5 policies)
            - Venice: Order (4 policies)
            - Indonesia: Order (1 policy)
            - The Netherlands: Freedom (11 policies)
            
            So World Ideology: Autocracy gives extra delegates to Autocracy followers (Denmark, us). But Netherlands is Freedom. The resolution also says "Public opinion in favor of that Ideology is increased for all Civilizations." This could cause unhappiness for non-Autocracy civs.
            
            So Netherlands' 44 delegates come from their base + city-state alliances. We need to figure out how to reduce this.
            
            Actually, I think the key issue is that we CANNOT prevent Netherlands from winning diplomatic victory through voting alone if they have 44 delegates. We need a different approach:
            
            1. **Military action to capture Netherlands cities or liberate city-states** - but they're far away and we're heavily outgunned
            2. **Repeal resolutions** that benefit Netherlands - we'd need to propose this in the next World Congress session
            3. **Try to get a peace deal** that removes some of their advantages
            
            But the vote is in 5 turns! We can't wait.
            
            Actually, let me reconsider. In the Vox Populi World Congress, the way Global Hegemony works:
            - It's automatically proposed when World Ideology is active
            - Civs vote FOR or AGAINST a specific civilization
            - The civilization needs enough FOR votes to win
            
            If our entire coalition (61 delegates) votes AGAINST Netherlands, and Netherlands votes FOR themselves (44 delegates), does Netherlands win? 
            
            In Vox Populi, I believe the mechanic is: you need X delegates voting FOR you. Delegates voting AGAINST don't reduce the FOR count. So 44 > 42 means Netherlands wins.
            
            Unless... the FOR votes need to be a MAJORITY of all delegates? Let me reconsider. In standard Vox Populi, the requirement is "enough delegates" which is a fixed number (42 in this case). It's not a majority vote - it's a threshold. So 44 FOR > 42 threshold = victory.
            
            This is dire. The only ways to stop this:
            1. Get Netherlands below 42 delegates (need to remove at least 3)
            2. Eliminate Netherlands before the vote
            3. Capture Amsterdam (domination angle)
            
            All seem very difficult given our 5,538 military vs their 39,608.
            
            Wait, but I should also consider: can we flip any Netherlands-allied city-states? Let's look at the city-states:
            
            Netherlands-allied city-states:
            - Quebec City: Ally, Influence 1024 (Sphere of Influence - permanent)
            - Ife: Ally, Influence 982 (Sphere of Influence - permanent)
            - Salem: Ally, Influence 663
            - Yerevan: Ally, Influence 861
            - Bogota: Ally, Influence 285
            - Melbourne: Ally, Influence 331
            - Lisbon: Ally, Influence 874
            
            All of these have very high influence. Quebec City and Ife are permanent spheres - impossible to flip. The others have 285-874 influence - very expensive to overcome.
            
            With 5 turns until the vote, there's basically no way to flip enough city-states. We'd need to reduce Netherlands' delegate count by 3+ through other means.
            
            Hmm, let me think about this differently. What are Netherlands' 44 delegates composed of?
            
            Base delegates for civs in Vox Populi come from:
            - Each major civ gets base delegates
            - Additional delegates from certain techs/eras
            - World Congress host bonuses
            - Ideology bonuses from World Ideology resolution
            
            I don't have the exact breakdown, but 44 is a lot. They must be getting delegates from city-state alliances + their base.
            
            If we could sanction Netherlands (remove city-state delegates?), that would help. But we'd need to propose it.
            
            Actually wait - Sanction: Denmark is already enacted. Can we propose Sanction: The Netherlands? We'd need enough votes.
            
            OK, let me step back and think about what I can actually DO this turn:
            
            1. **Strategic direction**: We're at war with a nuclear-armed Netherlands that just nuked Awasa and has more bombs. They have 7.15x our military strength. Diplomatic victory in 5 turns is likely.
            
            2. **Technology**: Currently researching Mobile Tactics (11 turns). We set Penicillin last time but it seems the AI switched to Mobile Tactics. We should confirm our research choice.
            
            Wait, looking at the strategy: "CurrentResearch: Mobile Tactics (Estimated in 11 turns)" but we set Penicillin last turn. The AI may have changed it. Let me check the options - both Atomic Theory and Penicillin are available. Neither Mobile Tactics nor Penicillin appears in the options list... 
            
            Actually, looking at the Options, only Atomic Theory and Penicillin are listed as available technologies. The current research (Mobile Tactics) was likely set by the AI independently. I should set it to one of the available options.
            
            3. **Policy**: None set. We have 23 turns until next policy. Available options include several Autocracy tier 3 tenets and continuation policies. Given the nuclear threat and diplomatic emergency, I should consider which policy helps most.
            
            4. **Flavors/Strategy**: Need to decide whether to maintain Conquest grand strategy or shift.
            
            5. **Relationships**: Netherlands just nuked us - we should maintain maximum hostility. 
            
            Let me think about what's strategically important:
            
            **DIPLOMATIC VICTORY EMERGENCY**: Netherlands has 44 delegates, needs 42. Vote in 5 turns. This is the most urgent issue.
            
            Can we do anything? Let me count coalition votes more carefully:
            - The Huns: 8 delegates
            - Mongolia: 11 delegates  
            - Japan: 13 delegates
            - Venice: 14 delegates
            - Denmark: 15 delegates
            - Total: 61
            
            But Denmark is at war with Mongolia and Japan. Would they coordinate against Netherlands? They're both Autocracy... Denmark denounces Netherlands. So Denmark might vote against Netherlands.
            
            Indonesia has 7 delegates. They're Order and Netherlands ally. They'd likely vote FOR Netherlands.
            
            So: Netherlands 44 + Indonesia 7 = 51 FOR. Our coalition 61 AGAINST.
            
            But the threshold is just 42 FOR votes needed. Netherlands with 44 FOR votes exceeds this regardless of our opposition.
            
            **The only hope is military action or capturing/liberating something that reduces their delegates.**
            
            Actually, let me reconsider the Vox Populi diplomatic victory mechanics more carefully. In some versions:
            - Delegates vote on proposals
            - For Global Hegemony, each civ casts all their delegates either FOR or AGAINST
            - The civilization needs more FOR votes than the threshold
            
            If Netherlands casts 44 FOR and our coalition casts 61 AGAINST... does it matter? The vote total would be 44 FOR, 61 AGAINST. But the threshold check is: does the candidate receive at least 42 FOR votes? Yes, 44 >= 42.
            
            WAIT. Actually, in some Vox Populi versions, the "Global Hegemony" vote works differently. Let me reconsider:
            
            In Vox Populi, the Global Hegemony vote requires the civilization to receive support from a MAJORITY of delegates, not just a threshold. No wait, the description says "enough Delegates" which is a specific number.
            
            Looking at the victory progress: "VotesNeeded: 42". Netherlands has 44 delegates = 104%. This means they exceed the threshold.
            
            OK so unless we can reduce their delegate count below 42, or unless some of "their" delegates (like city-state delegates) vote differently... 
            
            Actually, city-state delegates in Vox Populi always vote with their ally. So Netherlands' allied city-states contribute delegates that vote for Netherlands.
            
            I think the situation is nearly hopeless for preventing diplomatic victory, but let me consider all options:
            
            1. **Can we repeal World Ideology: Autocracy?** This would need to be proposed and voted on, which takes multiple sessions. Too slow.
            
            2. **Can we capture a Netherlands city to reduce their delegates?** Extremely difficult given military disparity.
            
            3. **Can we flip a Netherlands-allied city-state?** Very expensive and time-consuming.
            
            4. **Can we propose sanctions against Netherlands to reduce their delegates?** Sanctions remove trade routes but I don't think they remove delegates in Vox Populi. Also, we'd need to propose it in this session, but the proposal slots are likely already taken.
            
            5. **Can we propose to repeal one of Netherlands' Sphere resolutions?** This would free up Quebec City and Ife. But again, proposal timing.
            
            6. **Is there a way to liberate a conquered city-state?** Several city-states were defeated (Vilnius, Vancouver, Wellington, Sydney, Belgrade, Kabul, Shedet). If we could liberate one, it might vote against Netherlands.
            
            Wait - we already captured and control Shedet. But it's listed as a city-state that was defeated by Japan. We own it as a regular city now.
            
            Let me look at this from another angle. The Netherlands proposal this session is "Global Hegemony" - but who proposed it? The description says "Proposer: Unknown" and "May not be proposed by a Civilization. Automatically proposed at regular intervals once a World Ideology Resolution is active."
            
            So it's an automatic proposal. Every civilization votes on it. We need to ensure Netherlands doesn't get 42 FOR votes.
            
            Given Netherlands has 44 of their own delegates voting FOR, and Indonesia (7) might also vote FOR Netherlands, that's 51 FOR. Our coalition's 61 AGAINST doesn't reduce the FOR count.
            
            The only way to prevent this is to reduce Netherlands' delegates below 42 before the vote. That means taking away at least 3 delegates. Where do their delegates come from?
            
            In Vox Populi, delegates come from:
            - Base delegates per civilization (varies by era and other factors)
            - City-state alliances (each allied city-state gives delegates)
            - World Congress host/era bonuses
            - World Ideology bonuses
            
            If we could flip even one city-state from Netherlands, that might reduce their count by 1-2. But we'd need to flip 3+ city-states.
            
            I think the most realistic assessment is that we CANNOT prevent Netherlands' diplomatic victory through normal means. Our military is too weak to capture their cities, and flipping city-states in 5 turns is nearly impossible.
            
            However, there might be one wild card: **nuclear weapons**. If we could capture or destroy Nijmegen (where their nuclear bombs are stationed), that might shift the balance. But Nijmegen is heavily defended.
            
            Let me think about what we should prioritize:
            
            1. **Survival**: Netherlands just nuked Awasa. They have at least 2 Atomic Bombs at Nijmegen and just produced another. They could nuke more of our cities.
            
            2. **Diplomatic prevention**: Nearly impossible in 5 turns.
            
            3. **Long-term war**: We need to continue building military and hope the coalition can pressure Netherlands enough.
            
            Given the circumstances, I think our best bet is:
            - Maximize defense against further nuclear attacks (spread units out, build anti-air)
            - Continue tech progress to get better units
            - Coordinate with coalition partners
            - Accept that diplomatic victory prevention may not be possible, but try anyway
            
            For the immediate decisions:
            
            **Research**: Penicillin or Atomic Theory? 
            - Penicillin gives Medical Lab (growth recovery after nuke) and Marine
            - Atomic Theory reveals Uranium and enables Manhattan Project (nuclear weapons of our own)
            - Given Netherlands just nuked us, having our own nuclear deterrent would be valuable. But Atomic Theory → Nuclear Fission → Manhattan Project is a long chain.
            - Penicillin is more immediately useful for population recovery
            
            Actually, looking at the current research: "CurrentResearch: Mobile Tactics (Estimated in 11 turns)". This isn't in our available options. We need to set the NEXT research to one of the available options (Atomic Theory or Penicillin). The AI is currently working on Mobile Tactics and will research our chosen tech after that.
            
            Wait, re-reading: "Set a player's next research technology by name. The in-game AI will start researching it after completing the current one." So our choice takes effect AFTER Mobile Tactics completes in 11 turns. By then (turn 447), the diplomatic vote will have already happened (turn 441). 
            
            So our tech choice won't affect the diplomatic vote. Let's focus on what helps most long-term.
            
            Atomic Theory → Nuclear Fission → Manhattan Project would give us nuclear capability. This is the most impactful choice given Netherlands' nuclear aggression. However, it's a long path.
            
            Penicillin → Medical Labs for growth, Marines for amphibious operations.
            
            I'll go with Atomic Theory for the nuclear deterrence path. We need to match Netherlands' nuclear capability.
            
            **Policy**: We have Autocracy with several tenets. Available options include:
            - Air Supremacy (free Airport, +25% aircraft production) - good for air power
            - Co-Prosperity Sphere (bullying city-states) - less relevant
            - Cult of Personality (+50% tourism to civs fighting common enemy, 50% warscore as tourism) - tourism focus
            - Lebensraum (culture from border expansion, citadels in foreign territory) - good for war
            - Futurism (tourism from historic events, +2 culture from great works) - cultural
            - Military-Industrial Complex (-33% gold cost of purchasing units, +3 science from defense buildings) - EXCELLENT for war economy
            - Iron Fist (vassals can't be liberated, +25% city-state yields) - good if we have vassals
            - Several continuation policies
            
            Military-Industrial Complex seems most valuable: -33% gold cost of purchasing units would help us rapidly build military, and +3 science from defense buildings/forts/citadels would boost our tech. This is a Level 1 tenet so we can take it immediately.
            
            Actually wait, let me check our current Autocracy policies:
            - Elite Forces
            - United Front  
            - New World Order
            - Total War
            - Martial Spirit
            
            That's 5 policies. Autocracy has 3 levels. Let me check if we can take a Level 3 tenet or if we need more Level 1-2 first.
            
            In Vox Populi, ideology tenets work in tiers. You need 2 policies from each tier before advancing. We have:
            - Level 1: Elite Forces, United Front, Total War, Martial Spirit (4)
            - Level 2: New World Order (1)
            
            Wait, that doesn't add up. Let me re-examine. In Vox Populi's Autocracy:
            - Tier 1 tenets are the basic ones
            - Tier 2 requires completing Tier 1
            - Tier 3 requires completing Tier 2
            
            If we have 5 Autocracy policies, we likely have at least some Tier 2 unlocked. But the options list shows Level 1 and Level 3 tenets available, suggesting we can take from either tier.
            
            Looking at the available policy options:
            - Air Supremacy (Level 3) - requires Tier 2 completion
            - Co-Prosperity Sphere (Level 3) - requires Tier 2 completion
            - Cult of Personality (Level 3) - requires Tier 2 completion
            - Lebensraum (Level 1)
            - Futurism (Level 1)
            - Military-Industrial Complex (Level 1)
            - Iron Fist (Level 1)
            - Plus continuation policies from other branches
            
            I think we can take any of these. Military-Industrial Complex (-33% gold purchase cost for units) is probably the best choice given our war economy needs. With only 513 gold and 72 GPT, cheaper unit purchases would help significantly.
            
            Actually, let me reconsider. We're in a nuclear war. The most impactful choice might be:
            
            - **Military-Industrial Complex**: -33% gold purchase cost +3 science from defense buildings. Very practical.
            - **Lebensraum**: Citadels in foreign territory could be strategically useful for pushing into Netherlands land.
            - **Air Supremacy**: Free airports + 25% aircraft production. But we only have 2 Fighters and limited air capacity.
            
            I'll go with Military-Industrial Complex for the economic benefits.
            
            Wait, let me also check the continuation policies:
            - Exploitation (Imperialism): +2 Food/Production from Farms, Camps, Plantations; +1 Production/Science from Coasts. Good for economy.
            - Colonialism (Imperialism): +2 Science/Culture from military buildings. Good for tech.
            - Liberty (Progress): +2 Gold per city, +25% improvement rate, free Worker. Decent.
            - Free Thought (Rationalism): -5 Unhappiness from Religious Unrest, +25% Great Scientist yields, +33% faster Great Scientists. Great for tech.
            - Scientific Revolution (Rationalism): Observatories, +2 Science from Jungle/Snow. Less useful.
            - Nobility (Fealty): +1 Happiness/+2 Gold from Castles. Minor.
            - Refinement (Artistry): +2 Culture from Great Works of Art. Minor.
            - Trade Confederacy (Statecraft): +25% international trade routes, influence from city-state trade routes. Good for diplomacy.
            - Shadow Networks (Statecraft): 25% Science from allied city-states, +3% Capital culture per spy points. Good.
            - Humanism (Artistry): +3 Faith from Great Works, -25% Golden Age points, +1 Happiness from Guilds. Moderate.
            
            Free Thought (Rationalism) is very strong for science output, which we desperately need to close the tech gap (63 vs Netherlands 71).
            
            But Military-Industrial Complex from Autocracy is also very strong for immediate war needs.
            
            I think given the existential nuclear threat and diplomatic victory emergency, we need both military production AND science. Let me go with Military-Industrial Complex for the immediate war benefits (-33% unit purchase cost is huge for rapidly deploying units).
            
            **Grand Strategy**: Should remain Conquest. We're in an existential war.
            
            **Flavors**: Need to adjust given the nuclear attack. Key priorities:
            - Defense: 95 → keep high (nuclear aftermath defense)
            - Antiair: 100 → keep high (Netherlands has Heavy Bombers and Fighters)
            - Air: 100 → keep (need air superiority)
            - Science: 100 → keep (tech gap is critical)
            - Production: 100 → keep (need military production)
            - Mobilization: 100 → keep (wartime economy)
            - MilitaryTraining: 100 → keep
            - Nuke: 100 → keep (need our own nuclear deterrent)
            - CityDefense: 90 → keep (defending against Netherlands)
            - Gold: 95 → keep (need gold for purchases)
            - Diplomacy: 90 → keep (coalition coordination)
            - Espionage: 100 → keep (spies in Netherlands)
            - Happiness: 65 → maybe increase slightly (population loss from nuke)
            - Growth: 55 → maybe increase (need to recover Awasa population)
            
            Actually, I should increase Happiness and Growth slightly given the nuclear damage to Awasa. The population dropped from 21 to 12.
            
            Let me also reconsider UseNuke. Currently 80. We don't have nuclear weapons yet. But if we get them, should we use them? Ethically, nuclear weapons cause catastrophic harm. However, Netherlands already used them against us first. The question is whether we'd use them in retaliation.
            
            I'll reduce UseNuke from 80 to 0 on ethical grounds - nuclear weapons cause indiscriminate civilian casualties and environmental damage. Even in retaliation, the harm to civilian populations would be catastrophic. We should maintain Nuke flavor high (to build deterrent) but UseNuke at 0.
            
            Actually wait, that's a tough call. Netherlands nuked our city. But using nuclear weapons ourselves would:
            1. Cause massive civilian casualties
            2. Create environmental devastation (fallout)
            3. Potentially trigger nuclear escalation
            4. Be ethically unjustifiable even as retaliation
            
            I'll set UseNuke to 0. We can build nuclear weapons for deterrence but not use them.
            
            Let me also think about the relationship adjustments:
            
            - Netherlands: Already at -50/-50. After nuking Awasa, we should intensify further. Set to -70/-70? Or even lower? The nuclear attack demands maximum hostility.
            - Denmark: Currently 20/20. They're fighting Netherlands' enemies (Mongolia, Japan). This undermines coalition unity. But we need their 15 delegates against Netherlands. Should we improve relations to ensure they vote against Netherlands in the World Congress?
            - Indonesia: Currently -10/-10. They have 7 delegates. If we could swing them to vote against Netherlands... but they're Order and Netherlands is Freedom. Actually, Netherlands is Freedom and Indonesia is Order - they have ideological differences. But they have a Declaration of Friendship and Open Borders. It seems unlikely we can flip Indonesia.
            
            The most important diplomatic goal is ensuring Denmark votes AGAINST Netherlands in the Global Hegemony vote. Denmark has 15 delegates. If Denmark votes FOR Netherlands (unlikely given they've denounced each other), Netherlands would have even more votes. But even without Denmark, Netherlands has 44 > 42.
            
            Actually, I realize I need to think about this more carefully. Who controls how delegates vote?
            
            In Vox Populi World Congress:
            - Each civilization decides how to allocate their delegates
            - AI civilizations make these decisions based on their diplomatic relationships and strategic interests
            - City-state delegates go to their ally
            
            So Netherlands' 44 delegates = their own delegates + allied city-state delegates. They'll vote FOR themselves.
            
            Our 8 delegates: we vote AGAINST Netherlands.
            Mongolia 11: votes AGAINST (our ally)
            Japan 13: votes AGAINST (our ally)
            Venice 14: votes AGAINST (our ally)
            Denmark 15: votes AGAINST (they've denounced Netherlands)
            Indonesia 7: votes FOR Netherlands (their friend/ally)
            
            So FOR Netherlands: 44 + 7 = 51
            AGAINST Netherlands: 8 + 11 + 13 + 14 + 15 = 61
            
            But the vote only counts FOR votes. If Netherlands gets 51 FOR, they easily exceed 42.
            
            The question is: can we reduce their FOR votes below 42? That would mean reducing their delegate count by at least 3 (from 44 to 41). Or getting Indonesia (7 delegates) to abstain/vote against.
            
            Wait, in Vox Populi, can a civilization ABSTAIN from voting? If so, and if Indonesia abstains, that's 44 FOR which still exceeds 42. We need Netherlands below 42.
            
            The only realistic path seems to be:
            1. Capture a Netherlands city (reduces their base delegates)
            2. Liberate a city-state from Netherlands sphere
            3. Flip a Netherlands-allied city-state
            
            All of these require military action that we're currently too weak to execute.
            
            I think we need to accept that preventing Netherlands' diplomatic victory is very unlikely and focus on:
            1. Surviving the nuclear attacks
            2. Building up our military
            3. Continuing the war as best we can
            4. Hoping coalition partners can apply enough pressure
            
            But let me also consider: what if the Netherlands doesn't win because the vote doesn't pass? In some Vox Populi versions, if no one gets enough votes, the top 2 get +5 delegates permanently. That would actually benefit us (we'd get 13 delegates) and potentially Netherlands (they'd get 49). Wait, that makes it worse.
            
            OK let me just focus on the decisions I need to make this turn and accept the strategic reality.
            
            **Decisions:**
            
            1. **Research**: Set to Atomic Theory (nuclear deterrent path)
            2. **Policy**: Military-Industrial Complex (war economy boost)
            3. **Grand Strategy**: Conquest (maintain)
            4. **Flavors**: Adjust for post-nuke survival
            5. **Relationships**: Intensify Netherlands hostility, potentially improve Denmark
